No. n-3/84.—In exercise of the wwers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 28 of the Salar Jung Museum Act, 
1961 (26 of 1961), the Salar Jung Museum Board with 
the previous approval of the Central GovemmenL and in 
supersession of the Salar Jun.g Museum Board General Pro¬ 
vident Fund-Cum-Pension-Cum-Gratuity Rules, 1979, except as 
respects things done or omitted to be done before such 
supersession, hereby makes the following regulations, 
namely :— 

1. Short title and commencement :— 

(1) These may be called Salar Jung Museum Board 
General Provident Fund-Cum-Pension-Cum-Gratuity 
Regulation, 1983. 

They shall come into force from the date of their 
publication in the Official Gazette. 

2. Application of certain rules, to the employees of the 
Salar Jung Museum Board 

The General Provident Fund (Cenbal Services) Rules, 
19CT and the Central Ovil Services (Pension) Rules. 
1972, as amended from time to time, shall be applica- 
able to the employees of the Salar lung Museum 
■Board.

COUNCIL OF ARCHITECTURE 


(Incorported under the Architects Act, 1972) 

ANNUAL REPORT OF THE COUNCIL OP ARCHITEC¬ 
TURE (INCORPORATED UNDER THE ARCHITECTS 
ACT, 1972) FOR THE YEAR ENDING .HST MARCH, 

1983 

The Council of Architecture (Incorporated under the 
Architects Act, 1972) has pleasure in presenting herewith 
its Annual Report together with the audited statement of 
accounts, reviewing the progi-css for the year ending 31st 
March, 1983. 

Council of Architecture 

The Council met once on 19th October, 1982 at Goa at 
the invitation of the Government of Goa, Daman and Diu, 
during the year under report. 

The following arc some of the important points on which 
the Council deliberated on the recommendations of the 
Executive Committee :— 

1. Continued the action taken by the President for 
incorporating the suggestions of the Union Ministry 
of Works and Housing on the discuments on Archi¬ 
tects (Professional Conduct) Regulations, 1982 and 
Architects (Condition of Engagement and Scale of 
Professional Charges) Regulations, 1982. for forwar¬ 
ding to rhe Central Government in terms of the 
provisions of the Act. 

2. Confirmed the action taken by the President in re¬ 
wording some parts of the Minimum Standards of 
Architectural Education Regulations as recommended 
by the Special Committee and the Education Com¬ 
mittee without making any departure in the Regula¬ 
tions for forwarding to the Central Government. 

3. Approval of the Annual Report and the Audited 
Statement of Accounts for the period ending 31st 
March, 1982. 

4. Approval of the revised budget estimates for the 
year 1981-82. 

5. Creation of posts and upgradatiou-cum-revision of 
pay-scale. 

6. Confirmed the action taken by the President in mak¬ 
ing temporary ad hoc appointments in the Camp 
Office of the Council. 

7. Creation of Council of Architecture Gratuity Fund 
for pay nient of gratuity to the employees of the 
Council under Death-cum-Retirement Gratuity 
Scheme as is applicable to Central Government 
employees. 

8. Consideration of the report of the sub-committee to 
examine on the question of work-load, pay-scale and 
other working conditions of architects in public ser¬ 
vice. 


The Council regrets to note the sad demise on 14-3-83 
of Major Gen. Harkhat Singh who was the Council member 
since January, 1977 as a nominee of Institution of Engi¬ 
neers, India. Late Major Gen. Harkirat Singh, in addition 
to being an active member of the Council and its commit¬ 
tees his contribution and ratdance in professional matters 
was very significant. He championed the cliusc of profes¬ 
sionalism at various forums. 

II. Executive Committee 

The Executive Committee met three times during the 
year under report on 5-6-1982, 18-10-1982 and 25-3-1983 
and considered at these meetings the following important 
matters. The recommendations of he Committee taken til 
these meetings were placed before the Council at its subse¬ 
quent meetings for necessary acton 

1. Interviewed candidates for the post of Rcgi.stnu' in 
the Council of Architecture in the p-ay-scale of Rs. 
1500-60-1800. Selected Prof, D. C. Thapar, Princi¬ 
pal, Government School of Architecture, Lucknow 

' and offered the post of Registrar of the Council to 
Prof. D, C. Thapar for a period of one year in 
the pay-scale of Rs. 1500-60-1800 with the prior 
approval of the Central Government. 

2. TTic Executive Committee also decided that if Prof. 

, Thapar did not join witliin the prescribed time, the 

oflfer may be made to Prof. R. V. Kolhalkar, Head, 
Department of Architecture, College of Fine Arts 
and Architecture, Hyderabad, 

3. Creation and up-gradation-cum-rcvisioii of pay-scales 
of the various posts in the Office of the Council as 
per the decision of the Council. 

4. Approved tho revised budget csliimtcs for the year 
1982-83 .and tile budget proposals for 1983-84 for 
placing before the Council, 

5. Received the Inspection Committee reports on the 
inspection of : 

(i,i Sir I, 1. College of Architeciui'e, Eonibay, 

(ii) Academy of Architecture, Bombay. 

(iif) B.K.P.S, College of Architecture, Poona. 

(iv) Department of Architecture. Manipal Instiuue of 
Technology, Manipal. 

(v) Department of Architecture, University of Rooi- 
kce, Roorkec. 

Ivi) Department of Architecture, School of Planning 
and Architecture, New Delhi, 

6. Considered the observations/comments received from 
the authorities of the Schools/Departments of Archi¬ 
tecture at Madr.as, Trivandrum, Bangalore, Hydera¬ 
bad, Sibpore and Jadavpur together witii comments 
of the Convenors of the respective inspection com¬ 
mittees, Decided that a committee comprising Sliri 
lasbir Sachdev. who aclod as Convenor of the Ins¬ 
pection Committee and Sbri B. Kambo. should visit 
ihc Schools of Arehiicctuie)'Departments of Archi¬ 
tecture at Madras, Hyderabad, Bangalore and Tri- 
vandriun, to assess the follow-up action taken by 
the audiorities on the reports of the Expert Com¬ 
mittee so that the recommendations of ’this 2 mem¬ 
ber committee could be considered by the Executive 
Committee for recommending to the Council the 
course of action to be taken with the appropriate 
Governments in terms of the provisions of the Act 
and the regulations made thereunder. 

III. Admission Committee 

The Admission Committee scrutinised, h\ circulation, 26 
applications rcceivnd under seelion of tuc rici for re¬ 

gistration during the year under report. The work connected 
with semtiny of applications continues to he in progress. 

rV. Council of Architecture Regulations, t 1982 

The Council of Architecture Regulations, 1982, finalised by 
the Council, has been approved by the Central Govemmeat 
nad notified in the Gaz^e of India as required under the 
provisions of the Architects Act, 1972,

V. Council of Architecture {Minimwn Standarch of Archi¬ 
tectural Education) Regulations, 1983 

The docwneiit on Council of Archilecttire (Minimum 
Standards of Archilectural Education) Regulations, 1983, 
finalised by the Council, has been approved by the Central 
Government and notified in the Gazette of India as i-equircd 
imder-the provisions of the Architects Act. 1972. 

VT. Architects (Rrofessloiuil Conduct] Regulations, 1982 and 
Architects (Conditions of Engagement and Scale of 
Charges) Regulations, 1982 

Informal discussions by the President of the Council and the 
representatives of the Union Ministry of Education and Culture 
with the officials of the Union Ministry of luiw and Justice have 
taken place and the approval of the Central Government, for 
these documents is still awaited. 

VTl. Inspection of Schools of Architecture in India 

The Committee of inspectors comprising S/Sbri 
A. P. Kanvinde, Josbir Sachdev and P. M, TTiackcr inspectesl 
in January, )9R3 the Department of Architecture, Maiilana 
Azad College of Technology, Bhopal, The repiort of the inspec¬ 
tors on the inspection is stHl awaited. 

'I'hc Council is thankful to the authorities of the Maulana 
Azad College of Technology, Bhopal for the facilities and co- 
opcralion extended to the committee during inspection. 

Ihe Council resets to note tliat many institutions h.ave 
started/contemplatmg to start courses in architecture leading 
to (be award of recognised qualifications without the knowledge 
of the Council of Architecture and even without adequate 
infrastrucUire. Efforts are being made to inspect these institu¬ 
tions to maintain standards. 

The Council has drawn the attention of the University Grants 
Commission to impress upon the universities and institutions to 
seek guidance of the Council before startin.i; a course for im¬ 
parting training as architects. 


VJII. Registration 

The Council received 695 applications for registration 
during the year under report—659 under section 25(a) and 
36 under section 25(a) and 36 under section 25(b) of the 
Architects Act, 1972. Out of 659 applications received, 619 
have been registered under section 25 (h) and Original Clerti- 
ficates of Registration issued to them. The remaining 40 
applications under section 25(a) arc in various stages of 
scrutiny as in some cases documentary evidence in support of 
their claims for registration is awaited. Out of 36 applica¬ 
tions receivctl under section 25(bJ, J per.son vas registered 
under section 25(b) duiing the year under icporl :md one 
applicant was rejected as he was not found eligible for 
registration. The remaining 34 applications are in various 
stages of scrutiny. The number of architects registered during 
the year under report is 650. The statement indicating the 
position of lepisti-ation during the year under report and the 
total registration ns on 31st March, 1983 is indicated in 
Statement-I. ' 


IX. Registration Renewal /Restoration 

The registration fee of Rs. 40/- for the year 1983 was 
due from 5051 architects for letentlon of their names in the 
Register of Architects maintained by the Coimcil. 

Intimation letters to all the architects, from whom the 
renewal fee for the year 1983 was due, were sent. 

621 names of architects that were removed as defaulters 
due to non-payment of renewal fee during 1977, 1978, 1979, 
1980, 1981 and 1982 have been restored on payment of the 
prescribed restoration fee during the year under report as 
detailed in Statemeut-If. 

The response from archih;cLs for layment of renewal fee 
in advance to overcome the difficulties experienced by them 
for annuallv renewing their registration ba.s been encourag¬ 
ing,

Xi. Building Fund 

The Council has been able lo build up, from out of its 
meagre resources, a building fund of 'Rs. 6,50,750.60 ps. as 
on 31-3-1983 by an addition of a sum of Rs. 1,45,595.67 ps. 
to the previous vciir’s balance ot Rs. 5,05.154.93 ps. 

Xlf. Finances 

The Audited Statement of Accounts for the year ending 
31st March, 1983 is appended to the report. The Council has 
not so far been able lo repay the Central Government the 
interest free loan ot Rs. 1.50 lakhs. Eltorts to receive the 
grant-in-aid from the Central Government in spite of it.s best 
eft'orfs has so far not been successful. 

XIII. Litigation 

The writ petitions in tlio High Court of uetlii, filed by two 
applicants viz. S/Sliri O. P. Mittal and K. N. Pandey against 
the Council who had sou^t registration as architects under 
section 25(b) of the Architects Act, 1972, have been dismissed 
by the High Court. The decision of the Council for not register¬ 
ing them has been upheld and valid. 

XIV. Camp Office 

The Camp Office of tJic Council continued to function trom 
the premises of the Northern Chapter of the Indian institute 
of Architects. Efforts art still continuing to secure suitable 
accommodation for the office of the Council. The staff mem¬ 
bers of the Council continued to function from the Camp 
Office in spite of difficulties for space and facilities. 

EiTorts of the Council to have the Registrai in position have 
not yet raatoriallsed.

We have audited the annexed Balance Sheet of Council of Architecture, 8-B, Shankar Niarket, 
Connaught Circus, New Delhi-1 as on 31-3-1983 and also Income and Expenditure Account for the year 
ended on that date with the accounts books and vouchers as produced to us and do hereby certify that:_

and belief were necessary for the purpose of Audit; 

(ii) Balance Sheet and Income & Expenditure Account dealt with the Report are in agreement 
with the books of accounts; 

(iii) In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to explanations given lo 
in the accounts road together with notes thereon and documents annexed thoveon give-a true 
and fair view : 

(a) In the case of Balance Sheet of State of Affairs t^f the Council as on 31-3-1983; and 

(b) In case of Income & Expenditure Account of the excess of Income over expenditure 
for the year ended as on that date.
